### 🤖 Claude

**What A5 is:** the deployment story, not a feature. Four deliverables:

1. **`docker-compose.prod.yml`** — the whole stack in one file: postgres, redis, minio, mailpit, plus api/worker/admin/storefront and a Caddy reverse proxy. Healthchecks, `restart: unless-stopped`, env from a single `.env`, and the app containers made replica-safe (no container-local state — sessions already live in Redis, files in MinIO).
2. **`deploy/caddy/Caddyfile`** — wildcard tenant routing with automatic TLS: `admin.{$BASE_DOMAIN}` → admin, `api.{$BASE_DOMAIN}` → api, `*.{$BASE_DOMAIN}` → storefront. This is what makes "one deployment serves many shops" tangible rather than a claim in the README.
3. **GHCR image push** on `main` only — PR checks untouched.
4. A small `CustomDomain` fallback in the storefront tenant resolver (which is WS-E's file, so it goes to them as a snippet, not a silent commit).

**Is it OK this early?** Two honest sides:

*Against:* the issue file explicitly says "schedule for Day 2 — it must not steal Day 1 agents from features." And step 5 of the acceptance is "bring the stack up and complete a full purchase with the mock card" — that flow doesn't exist yet (no products API, no checkout, no payment router). So I'd be able to verify infra plumbing (containers healthy, Caddy routing to the right app, TLS, tenant Host passthrough) but **not** the end-to-end purchase. That part would have to be re-verified later, by me or by H3.

*For:* the "don't steal agents" concern is about opportunity cost, and right now there is no opportunity to cost — every feature issue is blocked behind A1/D1/D2/F1/G1, all claimed. Doing A5 now also de-risks it: Dockerfile breakage found today is cheap, found on Day 2 during the demo crunch it is not.

My read: it's a reasonable pick, but it is genuinely the weakest issue on the board and its acceptance criteria can't be fully met yet. Three alternatives worth your call:

- **A2 (tenancy suite)** — depends on A1, which exists as a branch with only a claim commit. Blocked in practice.
- **Wait ~30–60 min** for D1 to merge and D2 to land, then take **D3 (payment router)** — a load-bearing feature issue, and the one that unblocks C3/E3/D4.
- **A5 now, scoped honestly** — build it, verify everything verifiable, and state plainly in the PR that the purchase-flow leg of step 5 is deferred.

Which do you want? I'd lean **A5 now with the deferred-verification caveat** — it's real work that nothing else is waiting on, and idling an agent for an hour buys nothing.

### 🤖 Claude

Schema is complete (all domain models landed in `001_wsa_initial`), so H1 has no hidden blocker — the issue's "do NOT wait for B/C/D services" holds.

My recommendation: **H1**, for two reasons.

1. **Leverage.** "The seed is the demo" (SPEC §7). Right now every UI agent — B5 products, C5 orders, G3 analytics — will build pages against a database containing one shop and one user. They cannot do their manual acceptance, and G3's charts have nothing to draw. H1 unblocks all of that plus H2's five smoke flows. F2 improves storefront polish, which matters, but nothing is *waiting* on it.
2. **Collision risk.** The WS-F agent just landed F1 and left a handoff note pointing directly at F2's 13 placeholder files; their worktree is live and they hold all the context. They are the obvious next claimant. Nobody is on WS-H at all.

### 🤖 Claude

Here's where things stand while CI runs.

**Picked H1** (Aurora Supply Co. seed) once you confirmed F1 and D1 had landed — it was the highest-leverage unblocked issue, since every UI agent was otherwise building against a database containing one shop and one user.

**Built and landed as [PR #17](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/17)** (auto-merge armed, squash, branch delete):

| | |
|---|---|
| Products | 32 (30 active, 1 draft, 1 archived) / 133 variants, hand-written apparel copy |
| Orders | 40 over 60 days — 30 fulfilled, 6 unfulfilled, 2 partially refunded, 2 cancelled |
| Rest | 2 locations, 4 collections, 25 customers, 3 discounts, mock processor, published aurora theme, ~5.8k analytics events + 59 days of rollups |

Seed runs in ~5s; split into one module per domain so it rebases in small pieces.

**On TDD** — I wrote [seed.test.ts](packages/db/prisma/seed/seed.test.ts) first, and it earned its keep. Two real bugs it caught:

- `WELCOME10` is flagged `oncePerCustomer`, but five customers were redeeming it up to three times — data that would have made C1's engine and C6's UI look broken.
- The determinism check was comparing an unordered nested Prisma select, i.e. Postgres row order rather than content. That one was a bug in my *test*, not the seed — worth saying plainly, because my first fix attempt targeted the wrong cause (I hardened the date anchoring, which was a defensible change on its own but wasn't what was failing).

Rather than trust a green bar, I ran three deliberate mutations — a one-cent total drift, an inventory level drifting from its ledger, and dropping the once-per-customer guard. Each was caught by exactly the right assertion.

**Two honest gaps:**

1. The issue's acceptance also asks for a walk through the admin's Home/Orders/Products/Analytics. Those pages don't exist yet (A3, B5, C5, G3 unlanded), so I verified the layer that does exist: the data via independent SQL, and the storefront by rendering the seeded theme through F1/F2's engine — real product titles, images and `$220.00` reach the markup, and the hero screenshot above is that render.
2. The mock `ProcessorConfig` has null credentials rather than sealed dummy ones. `packages/db` can't import `packages/pay` to seal them — pay already depends on db, and I confirmed turbo rejects the cycle outright rather than assuming it. Logged in `DECISIONS.md` for D3/D4.

Main moved three times under me (A1, D3, F2, then B1); I rebased each time and re-ran the full gate. Last run: 10/10 turbo tasks green, 32 seed tests passing.

I'll report back when CI finishes and the PR merges — or if it goes red.

### 🤖 Claude

**H1 is merged.** [PR #17](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/17) landed on `main` as `b151cdf` — `pr-checks` green, squashed, branch deleted.

`pnpm db:reset` now builds Aurora Supply Co. in ~5s: 32 products / 133 variants, 2 locations, 4 collections, 25 customers, 40 orders across 60 days, 3 discounts, mock processor, published aurora theme, and 59 days of analytics with rollups backfilled.

What this unblocks immediately: **H2** now has one of its five dependencies met, and every UI agent (B5, B6, C5, C6, G3) gets a populated database to build against instead of an empty one. G3's charts have 59 days of data to draw.

Two things worth flagging for whoever picks up next:

- `seed.test.ts` runs the real seed against Postgres on every PR. If someone adds a required column without a default, that test goes red before their UI does — the fix is the seed module that owns the model, not skipping the test.
- The mock processor's null credentials are a deliberate, logged deviation from H1 step 8 (turbo rejects a `db → pay` cycle). D3/D4 should read "mock, enabled, no credentials" as the connected state.

Both are in `docs/AGENT-LOG.md` and `DECISIONS.md` along with the useful demo handles (`jane@example.com`, `WELCOME10`, collection `featured`, orders #1001–#1040).

Looking at the board now: **F3** (themes API + AI job) needs F1 ✓ and G1, and **G2** (analytics pipeline) needs A1 ✓ and G1 — so both hinge on whether G1 has landed. **B2** (files/MinIO) and **B3**/**B4** are now open behind B1 ✓. Want me to claim one, or stop here?

### 🤖 Claude

No — H1 was the only one, and I've landed it. Both remaining H issues are blocked, and not narrowly:

| Issue | Deps | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **H2** e2e smoke | B5, C5, E4, F4, H1 | H1 ✓ only. The other four aren't started or claimed |

H2's four missing dependencies all sit behind **A3 (admin shell)**, which nobody has claimed. So the H workstream can't move until a chain lands: A3 → B5/C5/F4 (+ E1→E3→E4) → H2 → H3. Realistically several issues deep.

Current board — landed: A1, B1, C1, D1, D2, D3, F1, F2, H1. In flight: C2 ([PR #14](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/14)), G1 ([PR #11](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/11)).

**Unblocked and unclaimed right now: A2, A3, A5, B2, B3, B4, C4, E1.**

Of those, **A3 is the one worth taking**. It's the single biggest bottleneck on the board — it gates B5, B6, C5, C6, D4, F4, G3, G4, and therefore everything H. It's also the KPI workstream: the Frame/TopBar/Navigation shell is what a Shopify user sees first, and `PARITY.md` is binding for it. Nothing else available unlocks more.

Want me to take A3? If you'd rather keep me on smaller API work, **B3** (collections), **B4** (inventory/locations) or **C4** (customers) are all clean half-day issues with deps satisfied — but none of them moves H forward.

### 🤖 Claude

**E1 — Storefront API + cart service.** It's the public, unauthenticated half of the API, tenant-resolved by Host header (`demo.lvh.me` → `request.db`) rather than by session:

- **Read endpoints**: `/storefront/api/shop`, `/theme` (the published `ThemeDoc`, plus signed preview), `/products` (+`/:handle`, `?query=`), `/collections/:handle/products` — active products only, with cache headers.
- **Cart service**: server-side cart referenced by an httpOnly cookie. Lines store `variantId + quantity` only; price/title/image are snapshotted *at read* from live variants, so a price change is reflected in an open cart — Shopify's behaviour. Totals via `money.ts`, subtotal only (shipping/tax/discounts are E3's).
- **Analytics beacon**: `POST /storefront/api/events`, insert-only ingestion.

## Recommendation: E1

It sits at the front of the **longest remaining pole to H2**:

```
E1 → E3 (checkout API) → E4 (checkout UI) → H2
```

Three sequential L-size issues. Every other H2 dependency is one hop from something already in flight — B5 needs A3 (in works) + B2; C5 needs A3 + C2 ([PR #14](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/14)) + C3; F4 needs A3 + F3 (waiting on G1, [PR #11](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/11)). The checkout chain is the one that stays long no matter what else lands, so starting it now is what actually pulls H2's date in. E1 also unblocks E2, E5 and G2 as a side effect.

Second reason, more practical: E1's whole test plan runs against the seed I just wrote — `curl -H "Host: demo.lvh.me" …/storefront/api/shop` returning Aurora Supply Co., draft products staying invisible (I seeded one draft and one archived precisely for this), deny-policy variants that can't exceed stock (I seeded six genuinely sold-out levels). I know that data cold.

The alternative worth naming: **B2** (files/MinIO, size M) is also on the H2 path and is half the size — but it's one hop behind A3, which someone else is already landing, so it doesn't shorten the critical path the way E1 does.

Shall I claim E1?

> #!/usr/bin/env bash
> #
> # worktree-env.sh — give this worktree its own database and Redis keyspace.
> #
> # Every worktree in this repo talks to ONE docker compose stack. Sharing a
> # database across them is not a tidiness problem, it is a correctness one: a
> # migration that is correct on its own branch (`ADD COLUMN ... NOT NULL`, say)
> # breaks every other worktree the moment it is applied, and `pnpm db:reset`
> # drops the database out from under whoever else is mid-test-run.
> #
> # So each worktree gets `merchant_<name>` and its own Redis db index. Both are
> # free: Postgres databases are catalog entries, not processes, and redis-server
> # already allocates all 16 logical dbs.
> #
> #   pnpm worktree:env

### 🤖 Claude

**E1 is built, verified and pushed** — [PR #30](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/30), auto-merge armed, CI running.

Nine endpoints under `/storefront/api`, all Host-resolved with no auth: shop, theme, products (+ detail, search, sort), collection products, the five cart operations, and the analytics beacon. 21 new tests, 133 api tests total, `pnpm verify` green.

**On TDD** — tests first, then implementation, then three mutations to confirm the suite bites: dropping the `status: 'active'` filter, checking stock against the increment instead of the line total, and skipping the preview HMAC. Each was caught by exactly the right test.

Three design calls I made rather than asking, all logged in `DECISIONS.md`:

- **Cart lines store `variantId` + `quantity` only**, repricing from the live variant on every read. It's Shopify's behaviour and it's what makes an open cart charge a changed price and silently drop a deleted product instead of carrying it into checkout.
- **Theme preview is HMAC-signed** over `SESSION_SECRET`. A raw `?preview=thm_…` would let anyone enumerate a shop's unpublished drafts. Invalid tokens fall back to the published theme, so a stale builder link shows the live store rather than an error.
- **Price and best-selling sorts rank in application code** — Prisma can't express either as a product-level `orderBy`. Correct at this catalogue size, and I named the one function to replace if that stops being true.

**One change outside my paths, worth your attention:** I raised Fastify's `pluginTimeout` in `apps/api/src/app.ts` (WS-A's file, one line, flagged in `AGENT-LOG.md`). I hit the real failure — right after `pnpm install`, on a cold Vitest cache, autoload's dynamic imports blew the 10s boot budget and the whole suite died with `Plugin did not start in time`. That's precisely CI's sequence, so it would have bitten there. It bounds startup only.

I also verified against the real seeded store rather than only in-process: full cart round-trip with `curl`, including the merge-then-oversell case returning a proper `409 conflict` with "Only 41 of Alpine Merino Crewneck are available", and the draft product 404ing.

Main moved three times mid-branch (B4 inventory, a B1 fix, and the PR-rebase tooling); I rebased and re-verified each time.

**What E1 unblocks:** E2 (storefront pages), E3 (checkout API) and E5 (customer accounts) are now open, plus G2 needs it. E3 is the next link in the chain to H2 — and it needs C2, which is still an open PR ([#14](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/14)).

I'll report back when #30 lands or if CI goes red.

### 🤖 Claude

Picking up where the session dropped: **E1 merged** as [#30](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/30) while I was working. But rebasing onto the newer main surfaced two real integration bugs, now fixed in [PR #36](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/36) (auto-merge armed, CI running).

**Smart collections rendered empty on the storefront.** E1 read membership from the `CollectionProduct` join table and left a note asking B3 to materialize smart membership there. B3 landed the opposite — resolved on read as a Prisma `where`, nothing stored. So on `main` right now, any smart collection a merchant creates shows the right products in the admin and *nothing* on the storefront. The seed hid it, because H1 wrote those join rows by hand. Fixed by calling B3's exported `smartCollectionWhere` — which their own comment says exists for exactly this. Proof, against the seeded store:

```
new-arrivals  | smart  | 0 join rows  →  5 products returned, productCount 5
```

**Two competing theme-preview token schemes.** I shipped `signThemePreview`; F3 landed `preview-token.ts` concurrently. Two HMAC formats over the same secret means the builder preview silently falls back to the published theme — a failure that looks like success. F3's is strictly better (shop- and version-bound, expiring, constant-time), so I deleted mine and adopted theirs.

Both `DECISIONS.md` and `AGENT-LOG.md` entries got appended CORRECTION lines rather than edits, since those files are append-only.

Two things worth flagging:

- I wrote the regression test first and it failed exactly as predicted — `expected [] to include 'alpine-merino-crewneck'`. Then two mutations to confirm the tests bite. One was instructive: I added an explicit shop-binding check on the preview token expecting it to be redundant next to tenant scoping. It isn't — the draft belongs to the shop being browsed, so a token minted for a *different* shop still resolves it without that check.
- I left `scripts/stack.sh` alone throughout. It was staged in this worktree but wasn't my work; it turned out to be someone's fork-bomb fix, which landed independently as #34.

Main moved four times during this (A2, B2, G1 with a schema migration, the stack fix). I rebased, applied the new migration, and re-verified each time — currently green at 194 api tests.

I'll report when #36 lands or if it goes red.

gh pr create --title "feat(ws-e): checkout API — lifecycle, shipping, taxes, complete [contracts]" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
Closes **E3**. The path where money moves: cart → frozen checkout → priced steps → charge → order.

| | |
|---|---|

### 🤖 Claude

**E3 is built, verified and pushed** — [PR #49](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/49), auto-merge armed, CI running.

Five endpoints, and one rule underneath all of them: every total comes from a single `computeCheckoutTotals`, and `complete` runs it once more server-side before charging. The client's numbers are a view, never an input.

**On TDD** — tests first, then implementation, then six mutations. All six were caught by the right test. But the more useful finding is what the *green suite missed*: after wiring everything up I drove a real purchase against the seeded store and read the resulting database rows, which surfaced three genuine defects the passing tests hadn't:

1. The order's inventory history included the **declined** attempt's reserve/release pair — a merchant would see a sale that never happened.
2. The customer's `ordersCount` stayed **0** after buying, because C2 records orders without touching the denormalized columns and C4 hasn't landed.
3. On a failure *after* a successful charge, the outer catch reopened the checkout — inviting a **second charge**. Now it stays closed; a merchant finishing one order by hand beats double-billing someone.

Each got a fix and a regression test that I then mutation-checked.

Two process notes worth your attention:

- I twice wrote a patch that silently didn't apply because Biome had reformatted the target, and once reported a mutation as "not caught" when it had never been applied at all. I've since asserted every patch and re-ran those mutations properly. The tax-base mutation *is* caught.
- Port 3001 was held by another worktree's dev server, so I ran mine on 3401 rather than killing it.

**Two deviations, both logged in `DECISIONS.md`:** a decline returns HTTP 200 with `{status:'failed'}` rather than the issue's "402-style SPEC error" — SPEC §5's fixed code set has nothing meaning "the bank said no", and the contract already models it as a response variant. And E3 maintains the customer counters itself until C4 takes over.

Main moved four times during this (F4, G1's queue wiring, a tenancy import lint rule, G2 analytics). I rebased each time, and removed my confirmation-email enqueue once G1 wired `notifyOrder` — it had become dead code.

**What this unblocks:** E4 (checkout UI) is now open, which is the last remaining dependency chain into H2 alongside B5/C5/F4. I'll report when #49 lands or if it goes red.

### 🤖 Claude

**E3 merged** as `c9caf31`. The checkout API is on `main`.

That's four issues landed this session:

| Issue | PR |
|---|---|
| **H1** — Aurora seed dataset | [#17](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/17) |
| **E1** — storefront API + cart | [#30](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/30) |
| — E1 integration fixes (B3, F3) | [#36](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/36) |
| **E3** — checkout API | [#49](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/49) |

The through-line worth naming: on both E1 and E3, the green test suite was not the thing that found the important bugs. Driving a real purchase and reading the resulting database rows found three defects E3's passing tests had missed — a declined attempt polluting the order's inventory history, customer counters stuck at zero, and a post-charge failure reopening the checkout for a second charge. Each then got a regression test and a mutation check. Same pattern on E1: rebasing onto B3 revealed that smart collections silently rendered empty, which no test covered because the seed had hand-written the join rows that masked it.

**Board state.** Landed: A1–A3, B1–B4, C1–C3, D1–D3, E1, E3, F1–F4, G1–G2, H1. That's nearly the whole spine.

The path to **H2** (the five smoke flows) now needs:
- **E4** — checkout UI. Deps E3 ✓ and D1 ✓, so it's **open now**, and it's the last unstarted link in the longest chain.
- **B5** (products admin UI) and **C5** (orders admin UI) — both need only A3 ✓ plus APIs that have all landed, so both are open too.
- **F4** ✓ already merged.

**E4 is the one I'd take** — same reasoning as before: it's the tail of the chain I've been building, and I hold the checkout semantics it has to render (the decline-is-a-200 contract, the per-click idempotency key, the inline `rejectedDiscount`). It's also a Polaris-free, Tailwind pixel-parity job against `PARITY.md`.

Want me to claim E4, or would you rather I take B5/C5, or stop here?

### 🤖 Claude

Important finding: Node's fetch **silently drops** an explicit `Host` header (undici treats it as forbidden) — the API saw `localhost` and 404'd. The hostname must be in the URL. That's exactly the trap worth a unit test.

🔧 **Bash**
```

> 25:STOREFRONT_PROTOCOL=http
> ```

```
cat > apps/storefront/src/middleware.ts <<'EOF'
/**
 * Carries `?preview=` into the layout (SPEC §12). Owner: WS-E.

 * exists to show.
 *
 * So the token is copied onto a request header, which layouts and pages can
 * both read. It is verified by the API, never trusted here.
 */

2026-08-28 | WS-E | The storefront's API client puts the shop slug in the URL hostname (`{slug}.lvh.me:3001`) rather than sending a `host` header. ### 🤖 Claude

To answer your first question directly: **E2 was not done** — the storefront was still a three-file placeholder. So I took it, and it's now [PR #53](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/53) with auto-merge armed.

That ordering turned out to matter: E2's own issue file lists **E4** as something it unblocks, and H2 flow (b) is "storefront browse → add to cart", so E4 would have been building a checkout with no way to reach it.

**Three bugs I only found by running the thing**, each of which would have looked like a data problem rather than a code one:

1. Node's `fetch` **silently drops** an explicit `host` header (undici treats it as forbidden, same as a browser). A header-based API client reaches the API as `localhost`, resolves no tenant, and 404s every page. I tested this assumption before designing around it, and it's the reason `api.test.ts` exists.
2. `?preview=` changed the *sections* but not the *theme tokens*, because Next doesn't give layouts search params — so F4's builder preview showed a draft wearing the published palette. Fixed with middleware that carries the token into a request header.
3. `/checkout` redirected to `localhost:3402`, dropping the shop subdomain, because `new URL(path, request.url)` yields the server's own origin.

**On tests** — only two unit files, and deliberately so. URL construction and the `Set-Cookie` relay are the two pieces that fail *silently* (every page 404s; add-to-cart does nothing for first-time shoppers). The rest is Server Components rendering the theme engine, which SPEC §14 forbids snapshotting — H2's Playwright flows are the mandated coverage there. Four mutations, all caught.

Two honest limitations:

- I couldn't click through the client islands: the preview pane blocks `/_next/static/*` (CSS *and* JS), so nothing hydrates there. I verified the cart and checkout glue over HTTP with a real cookie jar instead, and confirmed layout by inlining the stylesheet.
- I kept F1's `<img>` over `next/image`, against E2's perf wording — F1 documented that theme URLs are arbitrary remote hosts needing a per-shop allowlist. TTFB is 19–113ms on the production build regardless, well inside the 300ms budget.

Main moved twice mid-branch; the curated photography from #45 is what the storefront now renders.

**E4 is now the only unstarted link to H2** — `/checkouts/[token]` is the sole unowned route left under `apps/storefront`. I'll report when #53 lands.

### 🤖 Claude

**E4 — Checkout UI + hosted card fields + confirmation.** It's the screen the project KPI names explicitly ("checkout looks and behaves like Shopify checkout"), size L, Tailwind not Polaris.

Two routes, both under `apps/storefront/src/app/checkouts/**`:

- **`/checkouts/[token]`** — Shopify's two-column layout. Left: express-checkout placeholder row, Contact (email), Delivery (address grid in Shopify's exact field order + shipping method radios), Payment (card fields, "Same as shipping" toggle), full-width "Pay now". Right sidebar: line items with qty badges, discount input with inline error, subtotal/shipping/taxes, bold total — live-updating on every E3 `PUT`.
- **`/checkouts/[token]/thank-you`** — grey map placeholder, "Order #1001 · Thank you, Jane!", order summary repeated.

The load-bearing part is the **hosted-fields card entry**: a client component that POSTs `{number, expMonth, expYear, cvc}` *directly* to `{API_URL}/vault/tokenize` cross-origin, gets back `card_tok_…`, and sends only that token to E3's complete. Card data never touches the checkout server, never enters an E3 `PUT`, never reaches a beacon. That's SPEC §11 and a CLAUDE.md §9 landmine.

**Deps: yes, all done.**

| Dep | Status |
|---|---|
| **E3** checkout API | ✅ merged [#49](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/49) |
| **D1** vault | ✅ merged [#5](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/5) |

          {!addressComplete(address) || checkout.shippingOptions.length === 0 ? ("""
assert old in s
s = s.replace(old, new)
open(p,'w').write(s)
print('patched')

### 🤖 Claude

Two cross-origin `POST /vault/tokenize → 201` and nothing else card-bearing. Let me prove the PAN never reached our servers by grepping every log and the database.

🔧 **Bash**
```
PAN=4242424242424242; PAN2=4000000000000002
echo "=== PAN in any server log? ==="
for f in /tmp/e4apiL.log /tmp/e4sfL.log; do
  printf "  %-18s " "$(basename $f)"
  if grep -qE "$PAN|$PAN2|4242 4242" "$f" 2>/dev/null; then echo "LEAK FOUND"; else echo "clean"; fi
done
echo
echo "=== PAN anywhere in the database? ==="
pnpm db:query "select count(*) as pan_hits from vault_cards where \"encryptedBlob\" like '%4242%'" 2>/dev/null | tail -3
pnpm db:query "select brand, last4, length(\"encryptedBlob\") as blob_len from vault_cards order by \"createdAt\" desc limit 2" 2>/dev/null | tail -4
echo
echo
… [truncated, 473 more chars]
```

### 🤖 Claude

Everything holds:

- **No PAN in any server log**, and zero hits in the database — vault blobs are 56 chars of ciphertext, with only `visa/4242` and `visa/0002` metadata stored.
- **Order #1041**: 14800 − 1480 + 895 + 1132 = **15347**, and the payment captured **15347**. The $153.47 the sidebar showed is exactly what the card was charged.

Let me run the full gate and review the code.

🔧 **Bash**
```
pkill -f "next dev --port 3402" 2>/dev/null; pkill -f "tsx src/server.ts" 2>/dev/null; true
pnpm verify 2>&1 | tail -6; echo "=== build ==="; pnpm --filter @merchant/storefront run build 2>&1 | grep -E "checkouts|Compiled|error" | head -6
```

> **↳ result**
> ```
> @merchant/storefront:test: 
> 
>  Tasks:    10 successful, 10 total
> Cached:    8 cached, 10 total
>   Time:    3.059s 
> 
> === build ===
>  ✓ Compiled successfully in 5.3s
> ├ ƒ /checkouts/[token]                   5.43 kB         108 kB
> ├ ƒ /checkouts/[token]/thank-you         1.58 kB         104 kB

### 🤖 Claude

**Confirmed a serious bug.** On `http://demo.lvh.me:3402` — the documented dev URL — `isSecureContext: false` and `crypto.randomUUID` is **undefined**. "Pay now" would have thrown before ever reaching the vault. Fixing.

🔧 **Bash**
```
cat > apps/storefront/src/lib/random-id.ts <<'EOF'
/**
 * A random id that works on the storefront's real origins. Owner: WS-E.
 *
 * NOT `crypto.randomUUID()`: it is gated to secure contexts, and the documented
 * dev storefront is `http://{slug}.lvh.me:3002` — plain HTTP, so
 * `isSecureContext` is false and the function is simply undefined there. A
 * checkout that calls it throws on "Pay now" and never reaches the vault, while
 * working perfectly on `localhost` (which browsers treat as secure) and in
 * production behind TLS. That is the worst shape a bug can have.
 *
 * `crypto.getRandom
… [truncated, 2,097 more chars]
```

> **↳ result**
> ```
> patched
> apps/storefront/src/lib/random-id.ts:4: * NOT `crypto.randomUUID()`: it is gated to secure contexts, and the documented
> ok
> ```

### 🤖 Claude

… [truncated, 411 more chars]

### 🤖 Claude

**E4 is built and pushed** — [PR #61](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/61), auto-merge armed.

I read `PARITY.md` first as intended, and the layout follows it exactly: express placeholder → Contact → Delivery (country first, name pair, address, apartment, city/state/ZIP triple) → shipping radios → Payment → black Pay now, with the grey sidebar carrying qty bubbles and a `USD`-prefixed total.

**The security boundary holds, and I verified it rather than asserting it.** After a real purchase: two cross-origin `POST /vault/tokenize → 201` and nothing else card-bearing, both server logs clean of the PANs, zero database rows matching `4242`, and only `visa/4242` metadata stored beside 56 chars of ciphertext. `payForCheckout` takes a token — there's no parameter a card could travel in.

**The most valuable find was a bug I nearly shipped.** `crypto.randomUUID()` is secure-context only. On `http://demo.lvh.me:3002` — the documented dev URL — it's `undefined`, so **Pay now threw before reaching the vault**. It worked perfectly on `localhost` and would work behind TLS, which is the worst possible shape for a bug. I proved it on the real origin (old code threw `TypeError`, new code returns a valid key) and found E2's analytics beacon was silently hitting the same wall.

Two complete purchases through a real browser confirmed the flow: rates appearing only after an address, free shipping correctly withheld at $148 against a $150 threshold, WELCOME10 recomputing tax on the discounted base, a decline showing the red banner and staying payable, then order #1041 with the sidebar's $153.47 charged exactly.

**One judgment call worth flagging:** I skipped the `purchase` beacon that E4's text asks for, because WS-G's landed decision drops browser-sent purchase events as forgeable revenue and writes the real one server-side. Logged in `DECISIONS.md`.

On tests — one file, twelve cases, covering only the pure logic that fails silently (Amex 4-6-5 grouping, brand detection, `MM / YY` → `2030`). The rest is JSX that SPEC §14 forbids snapshotting, and H2's flows are the mandated coverage there.

**H2 is now unblocked on the E-chain.** Its remaining dependencies are B5 ✓, C5 ✓ (merged as #56 while I worked), E4 (this), F4 ✓ and H1 ✓ — so once #61 lands, **H2 has everything it needs**. I'll report when it merges.

### 🤖 Claude

**E4 merged** as `3e03b77`. Six issues landed this session:

| Issue | PR |
|---|---|
| **H1** — Aurora seed dataset | [#17](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/17) |
| **E1** — storefront API + cart | [#30](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/30) |
| — E1 integration fixes (B3, F3) | [#36](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/36) |
| **E3** — checkout API | [#49](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/49) |
| **E2** — storefront pages | [#53](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/53) |
| **E4** — checkout UI | [#61](https://github.com/pauljurgens11/shopify/pull/61) |

**Workstream E is complete** — the entire storefront and checkout path, from `/storefront/api` through the themed pages to a card being charged.

**H2 is now fully unblocked and unclaimed.** All five dependencies are done: B5 ✓, C5 ✓, E4 ✓, F4 ✓, H1 ✓. It's the Playwright smoke suite — the last mandatory blocking test suite in SPEC §14, and the gate to H3.

A note on what recurred across all six: the tests I wrote never found the most serious bugs. Running the real thing did. E3's declined-attempt inventory history and the double-charge-on-reopen window came from reading database rows after a real purchase; E2's smart collections came from rebasing onto B3; E4's `crypto.randomUUID` failure came from checking the *documented* dev origin rather than the one that happened to work. Each then got a regression test, but the finding came from the walk.

That's directly relevant to whoever takes H2 — those flows are where this class of bug gets caught automatically from now on.

Want me to take H2, or stop here?
